([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/dropout-identification-hypothesis-testing))

([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/dropout-imputation-benchmarking))
- **dropout-masking-benchmarking**: Use when a single-cell AS probability matrix (sites × cells) with NA entries is available and you need to quantitatively validate an imputation strategy (e.g., SCASL's weighted KNN) against held-out ground truth under graded dropout levels. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/dropout-masking-benchmarking))
- **dropout-noise-injection**: Use when benchmarking ST integration methods (e.g. stImage vs MUSE) on synthetic data that must include controlled transcriptomic missingness, or when reconstructing simulated ST datasets with distinct/ambiguous spatial patterns across 4, 6, 8, or 10 clusters. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/dropout-noise-injection))
- **dropout-noise-robustness-assessment**: Use when evaluating how a tumor/normal cell classifier degrades under increasing dropout noise in scRNA-seq count matrices with known ground-truth labels, or when reproducing F1 benchmarks like SCEVAN's 0.948/0.943/0.909/0.824 vs CopyKAT's 0.798/0.792/0.763/0.726. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/dropout-noise-robustness-assessment))
- **dropout-noise-simulation**: Use when you need ground-truth scRNA-Seq data with known DE genes to quantify how much dropout degrades differential expression (e.g. median t-statistic collapse) and how well imputation methods such as AutoClass, SAVER, scImpute, or DCA recover it. - **dropout-parameter-sweep-design**: Use when you must quantify how robust a cell-type annotation method is to dropout by sweeping Splatter's dropout.shape/dropout.mid parameters over simulated datasets with known ground-truth labels. - **dropout-process-modeling**: Use when scRNA-seq count data exhibit excess zeros beyond sampling zero (drop-out/dropout) and you need to model or test covariate effects on the zero-inflation probability itself, or to simulate zero-inflated count data.
